Rollbacks: the web alone, safe; the sync alone to rev 10, safe (rev-11 tabs re-learn the rev from each socket's contracts frame and decide by counting; `lb_receipts` stays in the board files, and a rev-10 room, its backups and its drop-legacy leave it alone, the last listing it as unknown); the api below 0024 needs `DRAFTROOM_ALLOW_NEWER_SCHEMA=1` (`schema-rollback.test.ts`). 0024 must not run while R0 can still come back (R0's api would pass the new values to R0's web), which is why all of this waits for the window. **Before deploying:** (1) licence: CR-2B-2 puts the port of tldraw 5.4.2's geometry (`getGeoShapePath`, `getPolygonVertices`; only `seededRandom` comes from MIT `@tldraw/utils`) into the web bundle, where until now it ran only in the server-side converter; C2 flagged it for licence review, so have the lead or owner look at it once. (2) Known limit: the first push after a reconnect (the flush of offline edits) goes before that socket's contracts frame, so it carries no receipt id and falls back to counting; answers after 5 s and trimmed receipts do too. (3) W5-53 stays open: an `@excalidraw/element` patch (head size by stroke width, the binding gap) through the catalog, guarded by `binding.test.ts` and `test:arrows`. (4) A browser case for "landed, ack lost, someone wrote after: theirs stands, told" needs a relay that drops the ack; it is covered in Node only (`receipts.test.ts`, `v1-receipts.test.ts`). Reproduced deterministically by `apps/web/src/canvas/sync/core/inflight-key-echo.test.ts` (2 tests, both fail without the fix); the silent half has been gone since `319d532`; the half still present at `release/held`, an echo write of the other user's element, is fixed by `bf45f19a` (**on `wave-c6/b11`, not in `release/held`**).   - **What wave E brings** (from each workstream's report and review; each fix has a test that failed without it, proved by the implementer and again by the reviewer by patch-revert). **E1 (api).** E1-1 closes wave D's D4 item 1: `redeemInvitation` (`routes/invitations.ts`), `email-invites.ts` `grant()` and the access-request approve insert the guest membership with `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE SET role = m.role` (a row lock that changes nothing), then read the multi-use `prior` grant `FOR UPDATE`, then insert the grant: membership, then grant, the order the member removal takes (`access.ts`'s lock-order comment gains the `workspace_members` step). For those three paths the removal vs redeem deadlock (one side's 500) and the orphan grant are gone. New real-Postgres tests pause the real routes midway (`test/race-support.ts`): `offboarding-races.pg` (5 tests; the removal-held-first case answered a real 500 with the old order) and `offboarding-email-grant.pg`. E1-2: a v1 checkpoint preview converts only within three bounds: `maxRecords` 1,000 (was 2,000); `maxBodyBytes` 2.5 MiB (the row's `size_bytes`, decided before the body is read, and the body's own byte length again before it is parsed); and, after the review timed 10.7 s for 100 notes of 24 KB inside the byte bound, `maxTextChars` 35,000 (label text counted in one pass: note, geo and arrow text in full, free text at 1/8, frame names not at all). Past any bound the api gives the existing "too large to preview" answer, remembered in the LRU; `checkpoint.preview.too_large` and `.slow` log the counts (records, bytes, textChars). The worst case the bounds allow measured about 285 ms on the 64-core desktop (2 MiB of strokes plus 35,000 characters of notes); a Render core is not measured. E1-3: DEPLOY.md's leavers line (the removal stays manual; the rest happens in its transaction) and the `408 REQUEST_TIMEOUT` / 60-minute ceiling note (Render's edge limit marked UNVERIFIED). **E2 (web).** E2-1 (D2): the shell's `onAccessLost` is a `useCallback` read through a ref, so a rename, role change or visibility change no longer fetches a ticket and starts a new board session (the role arrives through the room's close and reconnect, and the welcome). A guest's in-memory offline queue is per page (board + uid, `sync/adapter/offline-guest.ts`), so edits made while the session is closed survive a Retry; an access loss and an explicit sign-out (`createSignOutPurge`, CRIT-18) clear it. E2-2 (D5): `socket.ts` clears `awaitingPong` at the raw receipt of a schema-valid pong (still delivered in order), reports a heartbeat death and reconnects at once, and gives each socket its own frame chain. E2-3 (D3-2a): `session.ts`'s `select(ids, groups)` passes the groups to the engine (wave D's reviewed patch, unchanged); `selectedGroups` skips a group with fewer than 2 live members; `rekey-perf`'s 200-step case has a 30 s timeout; `history-selection.spec.ts` no longer skips itself. **E3 (api and web).** E3-1 (REVIEW-6, the api half): `access.changed` names the acting session's user for every flow that changed access (grant PUT/DELETE only when `changed`, request approval, review-link revocation, a visibility change to everyone on the board but the actor, workspace role PATCH/DELETE, email-invite grant, ownership transfer), and only for a real change; the unconditional `onResponse` hook in `events/routes.ts` is gone. The actor never reaches sync (`forSync` in the fan-out and the HTTP notifier; the api-to-sync body stays `{boardId, userId?}`). E3-2 (D6-1): the scaled-notch wheel guess (`100/ratio`, `120/ratio`) is off on macOS (`isMacPlatform`, `zoom.ts`); unscaled notches still zoom there. Not measured on a real Mac.

The api goes first so E1's race fix and E3's names are live before the web stops restarting the session on a role change; the web relies only on today's sync closing a session whose role changed. To watch: (1) After the api deploy, `checkpoint.preview.too_large` and `checkpoint.preview.slow` on staging. Legacy v1 checkpoints past 1,000 records, 2.5 MiB or about 35,000 characters of label text (roughly 270 notes of 130 characters) now show "can't be previewed here yet; restoring it as a new board still works" (wave D: up to 2,000 records); if the demo uses a legacy workshop board with long stickies, raise `maxTextChars` knowingly (about 50 ms of event loop per 10,000 characters of notes). (2) A visibility change now sends a named `access.changed` to every open stream on the board except the actor's, and each page does one extra role read. ### 2026-10-04
- **CI red on GitHub: unit shard 4/4 and Postgres shard 3/3 (branch `fix/ci-red`, worktree `Draftroom-ci`, from `origin/main` 878f8d93; pushed, NOT merged, NOT deployed; tests and harness only, no product code).** The pushes to `main` on 2026-10-03/04 ran CI (1,800 of the account's 2,000 free Actions minutes; 878f8d93 made it manual). Two jobs were red; the run logs were not available, so both were reproduced from the same shard split (vitest's `--shard` hashes the file paths, so a shard holds the same files on Windows and Linux). No workflow was run on GitHub. Three causes, all in tests:
  - **Postgres shard 3/3: `apps/api/test/grant-paths.test.ts` (wave F, never run on a real Postgres before).** Its `grantsOf()` read `ORDER BY user_id` and compared with an expectation sorted in JS by code unit. PGlite's text order is bytewise; the `postgres:18` image's database is `en_US.utf8`, where `'usr_b…' < 'usr_Y…'`. User ids are random mixed-case base62, so it failed whenever the ids' case order disagreed. Reproduced on Windows (Postgres 18.4, ICU en-US: both checks failed) and on Linux (`ubuntu:24.04` + `postgres:18` = 18.6 on an internal docker network on the NAS: one check, i.e. 1 error annotation + the exit code, the 2 annotations CI showed). Alone on `postgres:18`: 7 of 10 runs failed before, 0 of 10 after. Fix: the helper sorts the rows in JS (b44a8b43). Shard 3/3 at older pushes (23a36bbd, 61c039c7, b10aefa4, without grant-paths) passed on Linux.
  - **Unit shard 4/4, the likeliest cause: a race in the sync tests' raw protocol clients (42a51def).** Since ac72d87f (2026-10-01, SNAPSHOT_REUSE) a joiner within 60 s / 500 rows of the room's cached snapshot gets the welcome at the snapshot's clock, the snapshot, then a `changes` frame with the rows since it, and `contracts` last. `RawClient.hello()` (`element-room/support/client.ts`) and `ProtocolClient.hello()` (`harness/element-client.ts`) resolved on the snapshot, so `bound-arrows.test.ts` (in shard 4/4) could read `bob.confirmed.get('A')` before the catch-up landed (`SyntaxError: "undefined" is not valid JSON`). Seen once in a runner-like shard 4/4 at 23a36bbd and in 10 of 25 runs of that file with a CPU hog beside it on 2 CPUs at main's code; never on the 64-core PC. hello() now waits for the hello's `contracts` frame; 0 of 25 loaded runs fail. New `hello-catch-up.test.ts` replays the real room's frames with `changes` held back 300 ms: both clients failed it every time before. Not proven to be CI's failure (no log); every other shard 4/4 run on Linux passed (Debian `node:24.19.0` with `--init`, `ubuntu:24.04` with apt ffmpeg 6.1.1, a `runner` user, `CI=true`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`, cpuset of 2: shard 4/4 three times at main, once each at b10aefa4 and 61c039c7; shards 1-3 once).
  - **Unit shard 4/4 at 878f8d93 itself:** `scripts/ci-workflow.test.ts` still required the push/pull_request triggers 878f8d93 removed (red on every platform). It now pins `workflow_dispatch` only (59a30ac7).

Linux (NAS, `ubuntu:24.04`, 2 CPUs): shard 4/4 130 files passed / 5 skipped; api shard 3/3 on `postgres:18` 438/438 (shards 1/3 and 2/3 green at b44a8b43's code). Found on the way, not changed: a container without an init (PID 1 that does not reap) makes `office-sidecar.test.ts`'s "a worker that gives up" fail, because `process.kill(pid, 0)` counts the killed helper's zombie as alive (the runner's systemd and the deployed images' tini reap it); with workflow_dispatch as the only trigger, the `e2e` (`if: github.event_name == 'push'`) and `e2e-smoke` (pull requests) jobs never run; `protocol.ts` still says the room keeps its snapshot "for 5 s" (60 s since ac72d87f).
- **Excalidraw wave G: integration (branch `wave-g/integration`, worktree `Draftroom-wgint`, from `origin/main` 11ca0400 = wave F as deployed to staging, code e5cb6df, plus the test-only CI-red fixes; pushed, NOT merged to `main`, NOT deployed).** Merged with `--no-ff`, in this order: `wave-g/g1-vote-scope` cece13c6 (merge b613fe3a), `wave-g/g2-share-dialog` e7493347 (113d537b). **No textual conflict, and no file was edited by both branches** (G1: `apps/web/src/features/workshops/**` and one new e2e spec; G2: `apps/api` `features/dashboard/review-links.ts` and `audit.ts`, `packages/contracts` `dashboard.ts`, `apps/web/src/board` and `features/sharing`, two e2e specs).   - **What wave G brings** (from each workstream's report and review; each fix has a test that failed without it, proved by the implementer, again by the reviewer, and on the merged tree, below). Both were found on staging. **G1 (web only, workshops).** G1-1, vote scope after a new vote: a participant's `WorkshopStore` (`features/workshops/store.ts`) learned that a vote ended or started only from the board event stream, and nothing checked with the server when the person acted on its copy. With the stream missing the end of a selection vote and the start of a board vote, a guest joined the leftover join card and every click outside the old selection was refused locally. New `store.confirm(maxAgeMs)` (refetches unless the server answered within `maxAgeMs` and the last refetch did not fail) and `store.joinVote(sid)` (join, then confirm), used by the Join buttons in the overlay and `VotingPanel`. A click the tab's copy does not take (ClickToVote with the select or hand tool, and the vote tool in `ported/index.tsx`) goes through `refusedClick` (`ported/WorkshopOverlay.tsx`): a copy older than 2 s is confirmed first and the click decided again (cast if the open vote takes the object and the person joined it; the notice if not; nothing if voting ended or the open vote is not joined yet, whose join card shows). With every stream up the scope switched correctly both ways before the fix too (regression coverage, owner path included); the live owner-side report did not reproduce (the owner's store is updated by the answers to its own requests) and is covered by the e2e, including a note inside a still-selected frame. G1-2, vote catch-up after a stream drop: `refresh()` marked the state stale on a failed refetch and never asked again; the event that asked for it does not come twice, and a stream that stayed up sends no new hello (Chromium's offline emulation keeps an open SSE stream, so vote events arrived while every refetch failed). A retryable failure (network, 5xx, 429, an answer that is not the api's) is now retried after 1, 2, 4, 8 s, then every 15 s, until a refetch succeeds; any later success cancels it and resets the backoff; 401/403/404 and other refusals are not retried; a failure older than a refetch that already succeeded changes nothing; retrying stops while nothing subscribes (board left), resumes on the next subscribe and ends on dispose. `events.ts` is unchanged: a plain stream cut was already caught by the reconnect hello's refetch (that test passes before and after). New traffic: GET `/api/boards/:bid/workshop` on Join, on a refused click whose copy is older than 2 s, and on the retries. **G2 (api + web, Share dialog).** G2-1, stuck "Creating…": Create no longer waits on the clipboard. It ends when the api answers (button freed, link listed at once); the copy runs afterwards through the new `board/share-copy.ts` `copyBounded` (1.5 s limit) and only picks the sentence. Refused or timed out, the row says "Couldn't copy — use Copy." and the status line says so. Every Copy button in the dialog uses it; a null/false mix-up was fixed so only a link this dialog just created shows the note. G2-2, the name-only box on a link that exists: new `PATCH /api/boards/:bid/review-links/:id` `{ allowGuests }` (strict body; contracts `UpdateReviewLinkInput`, `ReviewLinkUpdated`). Only the flag changes (the link's role, expiry and uses do not, so guests keep the link's role on this board only). Managers only, never a name-only guest; turning it ON is creator-only (in-route `requireCreator`, domain access), turning it OFF stays open to every manager; an expired, revoked or swept link answers 409 `LINK_CLOSED`; board row `FOR UPDATE`, then the link `FOR UPDATE` (the order of +1 h, revoke and join); audited as `invitation.update` (a new value in `audit.ts`'s union; the column is plain text, no migration); `sharing.changed` reason `review-link-updated`; a no-op writes and publishes nothing. Turning it off stops new name-only joins; people already in keep access until the link ends or is revoked, so **POST `.../extend` now extends guest sessions whether or not the flag is still on** (behaviour change). Web: every workshop and client link row has its own "Anyone with this link can join with just a name (no account)" box with a status line; an api error shows on the row and leaves the box unchanged; the box above Create reads "For the next link: …" once a link of that kind is open. Non-creator managers see the row box only on links that currently allow guests (they may only turn it off).

On staging on 2026-10-05 the stream through the static site's `/api/*` rewrite delivered nothing to the browser, so live events only arrived with the client's 40 s stale-stream reconnect. Merged with `--no-ff`: `wave-h/h1-direct-events` 0ac4716e (merge 607cfb17), the wave's only branch. **No conflict.** No dependency or lockfile change (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` clean).   - **What wave H brings (H1: api, web and `render.yaml`).** The page opens the board event stream on the api's own origin instead of through the rewrite.
    - **Ticket mint.** New `POST /api/boards/:bid/events/ticket` (`apps/api/src/events/tickets.ts`, `routes.ts`). It is same-origin, so the session cookie, the api's Origin check and view permission apply. It answers `{ url, ticket, expiresAt }`. It answers `{ url: null }` when the new optional api variable **`DRAFTROOM_EVENTS_ORIGIN`** is unset (`config.ts`: an origin only, https in production). A ticket is `evt_` plus 32 random bytes (base64url). It is held only in the api's memory, single use, valid for 30 s and bound to one board. At most 4 wait per session (a 5th drops the oldest) and 10,000 per process (past that the mint answers 503 `TOO_MANY_SUBSCRIBERS`). Tickets are dropped when their session is revoked. Mints are rate limited per person with the stream-open budget, through a separate limiter. A ticket fails the session cookie's format check and is not a sync ticket.
    - **Ticketed stream.** `GET /api/boards/:bid/events` also takes `Authorization: Bearer <ticket>`. A root `onRequest` hook consumes the ticket and runs the request as the minting session. It is registered after the cookie parser and the Origin check, and before `registerFeatures`. Everything after it is the unchanged path: `requireUser`, the session check, `opens.take`, `requireBoardAction(view)`, `hub.openStream`, the 10 s revalidation, `endSessions`, the per-person caps, the guests' per-link stream cap and the workshops' raised-hand tracking. A request with an Authorization header is ticketed or refused, never let in by a cookie. A ticket in a query string is never read. The api keeps the minting session's cookie value with the ticket for at most 30 s and never logs it.
    - **CORS, on that one route, only with the variable set.** `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` is exactly `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`, with `Vary: Origin` and never credentials, and is also sent on the 401/404/503 refusals. `OPTIONS` answers 204 for GET with `authorization`, max-age 600.
    - **Web (`apps/web/src/features/events.ts`).** Every attempt mints a fresh ticket, then `fetch`es the minted URL with the Bearer header and `credentials: 'omit'`. The body is parsed like an EventSource would parse it (`createSseParser`). Hello/resync, the 40 s stale timer, the 1–30 s backoff and the server's `retry` (60 s for `replaced`) work as before. The page uses the unchanged cookie `EventSource` in three cases: on `{ url: null }`; on a 403/404 from the mint (an older api, or no access); and, for the rest of the page's life, after 3 direct attempts in a row end before hello. A direct attempt ends that way when the fetch is rejected (CSP, CORS, DNS), the ticket is refused, the answer is not an event stream, or no hello comes within 40 s. `LobbyBoardPage` is unchanged.

The api origin must stream `/api/diag/stream` with a first byte under 1 s (FAIL otherwise; the web origin's timing is INFO). A signed-out mint through the web origin must be 401. The api must answer the stream's preflight for the web origin. A made-up ticket must get a 401 the page can read. The CSP line now requires the api origin in `connect-src`. The implementer ran it only against a local api and `vite preview`, never against staging.

(2) Deploy the api build with `DRAFTROOM_EVENTS_ORIGIN=https://draftroom-staging-api.onrender.com` on `draftroom-staging-api`. (3) Deploy the web build. Then, in a real browser, a vote or comment should show on another tab within about a second.

A web deployed before the api gets 404 from the mint and uses the cookie stream. If the CSP lags, a new page fails 3 direct attempts and falls back to the rewrite, which is today's behaviour; the browser console shows the violations (the CSP has no report endpoint).

    - To watch: (a) One extra POST per stream attempt (the mint). (b) A zero-downtime api deploy runs two api instances for about a minute, and tickets live in one process. A mint answered by one instance and a GET answered by the other gets a 401, which counts as a failed direct attempt. Three in a row put that page on the cookie stream (events up to 40 s late) until it is reloaded. How Render splits traffic during the overlap is UNVERIFIED; see follow-up (2). (c) A page that fell back logs a `console.warn` starting "Lobby: live board updates use the page's own origin from now on".
  - **Follow-ups (the reviewer's minor findings and the workstream's notes; outside H1's files, not applied here; none blocks the deploy).**
    - (1) A mint that keeps failing with anything other than 403/404 is retried with backoff forever and never falls back. Examples are a persistent 5xx, 405, 415, or 503 from a full ticket store. A bug in the new mint route alone would then leave the page with no event stream at all, which is worse than before H1. The web test pins this behaviour on purpose. Fix: count those failures and fall back after a few, or after about 2 minutes.
    - (2) The fallback lasts for the page's lifetime, and the counted failures include transient ones: the api restarting between the mint and the GET, a ticket from an overlapping deploy instance, a DNS blip after wake. A long-open demo tab can end up on the degraded rewrite path with only a console warning. Fix: retry the direct transport after a few minutes, or on the next hello-less reconnect.
    - (3) Documentation outside H1's files. `.env.example` lacks `DRAFTROOM_EVENTS_ORIGIN`, although DEPLOY.md says every variable is documented there; H1's report has the two lines to add after `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`. `scripts/smoke/README.md`'s live-smoke table lacks the section-5b lines and the `connect-src` line. `STAGING_POLICY` in `e2e/spike/csp/suite-audit-policy.ts` lacks the api origin in `connect-src`; update it before the CSP audit is run again.
    - (4) The lead should accept knowingly that the brief's `VITE_DRAFTROOM_API_ORIGIN` became the api-side `DRAFTROOM_EVENTS_ORIGIN`: the variable goes on the api service, not on the web build. Deployments that leave it unset (dev, NAS) make one harmless extra mint POST per board open.
    - (5) A throwing `onResync` or `onAccessLost` listener ends a direct connection, because `handle()` runs inside the read loop's `try`. During the hello it also counts as a failed direct attempt. On the cookie transport it is only an uncaught error in `onmessage`. `emit`'s listeners are already guarded. Fix: guard those two loops the same way (a reviewer note).
    - (6) Check the direct transport in Safari/iPad and Firefox. The cross-browser smoke does not cover live events.

No dependency or lockfile change (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` clean). No migration (the api stays at `0024`). No `canvas-schema` code, `packages/db`, `packages/contracts` or sync/wire change; `CONTRACTS_REV` stays 11. `apps/sync` and `packages/**` change in two comments only (`packages/canvas-schema/src/protocol.ts`, `apps/sync/tsconfig.node-safe.json`); `apps/worker`, `render.yaml` and `.env.example` are unchanged. `origin/main` gained 2972c7d7 (`docs/GO-LIVE-AZURE.md`, a new file) after the base; it is not in this branch and touches no file of wave I. Trailers: the I1 and I2 commits carry `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5` (the model that ran them, as their reports say); the I3 commits and the three merges carry the Opus line. History was not rewritten.
  - **What wave I brings.**
    - **I1, live updates (web only: `apps/web/src/features/events.ts`, `features/workshops/private-notes/store.ts`).** Closes wave H's follow-ups (1) and (2) (BACKLOG EVT-1, EVT-2), and half of (5) (EVT-3).
      - (1) A failing ticket mint is told apart by its status. 0, 401 and 429 are never counted. 403 and 404 still mean the cookie stream at once. Another 4xx (400, 405, 415) counts like a failed direct attempt (3 in a row: the cookie stream). A 5xx counts by time: the cookie stream once failures have lasted `MINT_TROUBLE_MS` (120 s) with no good mint, so an api restart or deploy (5xx from the proxy for a few seconds) is waited out. A throwing resync listener no longer fails the stream (try/caught). A throwing `access-lost` listener is still unguarded (the other half of EVT-3).
      - (2) The cookie fallback is temporary. After any fallback (including the quiet ones: no direct origin, 403/404) the direct stream is retried after `DIRECT_RETRY_MS` (5 min), doubling per failed retry up to `DIRECT_RETRY_MAX_MS` (30 min), reset by a direct hello; at once on `online`; on `visibilitychange` to visible once the wait is over. A retry mints a ticket FIRST while the cookie stream runs; only a ticket with a direct origin swaps the stream, as one attempt with `PROBE_HELLO_MS` (15 s) to say hello, and one failure goes back to the cookie stream. No second stream is opened in parallel (the per-person cap of 5 would push out another tab). Pages built with `mintTicket: null` never retry. The console warning now reads "Lobby: live board updates use the page's own origin for now (…); the direct stream is tried again later." once per fallback (wave H's entry quotes the old "from now on" text).
      - (3) Faster catch-up. On `online` and on visible, a connection waiting out its reconnect backoff opens at once and the backoff restarts at 1 s; never a wait the server set (`replaced`, 60 s) and never a connection opened less than `WAKE_MIN_GAP_MS` (1 s) ago. Beyond the brief, accepted by the reviewer: `online` also replaces a stream that looks fine (Chromium's offline emulation, like a real network change, leaves the stream silent with no close), and visible replaces one that has been silent for the stale timeout, never one that delivers pings. The header comment of `events.ts` is rewritten for (1)-(3).
      - (4) The private-notes store retries a failed refetch like `workshops/store.ts` (1, 2, 4, 8 s, then 15 s; stops on 401/403/404; only while something subscribes; `subscribe()` re-arms it; `dispose()` ends it). A successful action answer also confirms the store, and `failedSeq`/`confirmedSeq` keep an older answer from clearing a newer failure. It imports `retryableError`, `REFRESH_RETRY_MIN_MS` and `REFRESH_RETRY_MAX_MS` from `workshops/store.ts`, which must keep exporting them.
      - Extra traffic: one same-origin ticket POST per retry while a page is on the fallback (at most every 5 min, backing off to 30 min) and one fresh stream per `online`. An I1 page against an api without the mint route or without `DRAFTROOM_EVENTS_ORIGIN` makes that one cheap POST per retry and stays on the cookie stream.
      - Review: all 4 items "good"; 2 minor findings, in the follow-ups.
    - **I2, creator grant lock order and the guest page queue (api and web).** Closes BACKLOG OFF-3 and GQ-1 (wave F's follow-up on `releaseGuestQueue`).
      - (1) New `requireCreatingRoleLocked(q, wid, uid)` in `apps/api/src/access.ts`, used by `POST /api/workspaces/:wid/boards` and `createBoardFrom` (`features/history/new-board.ts`: duplicate, restore as new, import). It peeks at the role without a lock (a malformed id or a stranger gets 404 and a member who may not create, a guest say, gets 403, all before any lock), then takes the workspace row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, then reads the role again, and that read decides (removed meanwhile: 404; demoted: 403). A member removal takes the workspace row first, so a creator removed while creating now gets 404, or the board is created first and the removal sweeps its owner grant; never a board whose owner grant outlives the membership. `access.ts`' lock-order comment gains step 0 (the workspace row). The review's major finding (the first version sent the raw `:wid` into SQL, so `%00` ids answered 500 instead of 404) and its guest-takes-the-lock minor are fixed in cc79df71. Cost: one more single-row read per creation.
      - (2) `LobbyBoardShell` (`LobbyBoardPage.tsx`) releases a guest's page queue when the board page unmounts, through `guestQueueLeaver()` and `releaseGuestQueueSoon` (`canvas/sync/adapter/offline-guest.ts`: a `setTimeout(0)`, so it runs after the session's `dispose`/`persistNow`, which React runs after the parent's cleanup; a remount on the same board by the same person before the timer, StrictMode's, cancels it). A Retry or a role change keeps the shell and releases nothing. Belt and braces: a non-empty save to a guest queue marks it a guest's queue even just after a release, so a late save cannot strand an edit. No committed test proves that the shell calls `guestQueueLeaver` (a review minor, in the follow-ups); the implementer's browser check of the wiring was throwaway.
      - Review: item 1 "needs-work" (the 500 above), fixed and re-proved by the implementer; item 2 "good".

History was not rewritten.
  - **What wave J brings.**
    - **J1, people outside Dark Burn / TPG (api, web, contracts; migration `0026_outside_markers`, was 0025).** (1) Manager-only outside fields (`outside: true | false | 'unconfirmed'`, the email domain and a masked address) on access requests, the Share dialog's members, the workspace member list for owners and admins and, with `?boardId=`, for a manager of that board; `User` stays clean; new `GET /api/boards/:bid/sharing/outside` and `POST .../sharing/address-check` (manage_access and a creator; 30 per caller per 10 min). (2) "External" tags on cursors, the people bar and list, comment authors, the @mention picker and the Share dialog. (3) One-time confirms before inviting or raising an outside person (the server's address check decides; any failure but a 404 sends nothing). (4) "Shared outside" on the top bar and the dashboard cards. (5) "Internal only" switches for a workspace and a project (`workspaces.internal_only`, `projects.internal_only`): 409 `INTERNAL_ONLY` for outside invitations, approvals, grants and raises, links, and accepting older invitations or links; nothing is removed, and the remaining outside access (people, links, pending invitations) is listed with Remove/Revoke. (6) The `outsider_joined` bell item for a board's owners the first time a known outside account opens the board (once per board and person, advisory lock; only to a web declaring `LOBBY_CLIENT_REV` >= 2, which goes 1 -> 2). (7) The team for team-only comments is role AND confirmed creator; every thread route and the bell confirm the caller first. (8) `cleanDisplayText` (controls, bidi, zero-width; ZWJ/ZWNJ kept between non-ASCII characters) on access-request messages and dev sign-in names on input, and on the names and messages owners read. Review: 2 major (the picker and typed @handles of a board owner who is a plain workspace member got no outside fields; a pre-switch invitation could still be accepted under Internal only), 5 minor, 1 nit; fixed in 5 commits (530c24a2 and the four before it). Still partial: Clerk display names are not cleaned on input (`auth/users.ts`, outside J1's ownership; follow-up).
    - **J2, access lifecycle (api only).** A name-only guest's grant ends with the last open link of theirs on the board, whatever role an owner gave them since (INV-2); removing someone from a workspace or a board revokes the unused single-use invitations locked to their addresses (INV-3) and denies their pending request for that board (INV-5); a raise by a second link of a grant a link created ends with the links (MISS-1); "Duplicate, keep sharing" copies no access a link gave (INV-4); at most 50 new access requests per board per day, the rest swallowed with the same 202 (INV-6). Review: every item good; minors left as decisions (the guest-only scope of INV-2, the silent swallow past the cap, no `sharing.changed` event from the board removal, the keep-sharing read window).
    - **J3, realtime security (api, sync, web store).** RT-01: every way a session ends (logout, Sign out everywhere, a sign-in replacing the browser's session, a Clerk ban seen by the identity refresh) tells the sync (`POST /internal/access-changed {userId}`), and `GET /internal/access` answers `signedInSince`; the sync closes the person's board sockets 4099 NOT_AUTHENTICATED when no session is live and 4000 RECONNECT for a ticket older than every live session. RT-03: during an open vote every count anyone sees is the last finished 5 s window's, and the sync relays a peer's `button` as `up`. Review: both good; documented limits: per person, not per session (tickets carry no session id); a ban is seen at the next identity refresh; a window with one cast still says someone voted.
    - **J4, DoS hardening (api, sync).** A linear link-preview head parser and reader (a 512 KiB hostile page held the loop 13-19 s before), c-ares lookups with time limits and a 6-slot preview queue (503 `LINK_PREVIEW_BUSY`); v1 imports (sync seed) and v1 checkpoint previews (api) converted in worker threads with a heap sized by the body (48 MB + 64 MB per MiB, ceiling 256 MB), an RSS gate on the seed worker and 413 `IMPORT_TOO_LARGE` when memory runs out; checkpoint byte caps (256 MiB manual and 1 GiB total per board, a workspace allowance, `DRAFTROOM_CHECKPOINT_WORKSPACE_MB`). Review: 2 major (the first flat 128 MB heap refused stroke-heavy boards that imported before, and the preview's "bounds imply the heap fits" was false), fixed in 62faad76 and fa493a3b; the workspace knob in edd0a4e4. Left: a real public-DNS preview on staging (LP-DNS-1).
    - **J5, "Send feedback" (api, web, contracts; migration `0027_feedback`, was 0026).** A board-menu and account-menu dialog with a visible context block; `POST /api/feedback` (a session or a guest session; authorship from the session; cleaned plain text; 10 an hour per person, 100 per client address); the inbox at `/feedback` and `PATCH /api/feedback/:id` only for verified addresses in `DRAFTROOM_FEEDBACK_ADMINS` (everyone else gets a 404); `GET /internal/feedback` behind the internal secret. Review: 1 major (a quadratic trailing-blank regex on refused, unthrottled messages pinned the loop about 146 ms per 8 KB request) and 2 minor (invisible tag characters kept; a late "Load more" landing in the other filter), fixed in 28714952 and 7d4f583e.
    - **J6a, polish (web, engine).** W5-53/ARROW: one pnpm patch of `@excalidraw/element` 0.18.0-1118751: an orbit bound end sits 1 + stroke/2 off its target (was 5 + stroke/2) and arrowheads scale with the stroke; every copy (web, sync, canvas-schema) resolves the patched one.     - **IO-5 (major): "+1 h" and "allow guests" on a review, workshop or guest link were refused only by the root hook**, which reads the switch before the route's transaction, and the routes never read it again: a switch turned on, or a move into an internal-only project, committed between the hook's read and the route gave an open link more time or opened it to name-only guests (a creator's stale identity check that a background refresh finished in between skipped the hook too). Both routes now take the workspace row (`lockBoardWorkspace`, after an unlocked manager check, before the board row) and decide with `assertLinksAllowed` in their transactions (PATCH only when turning guests on; turning them off is never refused). Creating a link took the board row alone while it read the switch, so a switch-on could commit in between (minor, finding 3); it now takes the workspace row first too. Lock order workspace -> board -> link, as a redemption. Tests: `internal-only-links.test.ts` (a switch-on and a move-in committed right after the hook's read: 4 failed before), `internal-only-locks.pg.test.ts` (f): a new deferred trigger records a link created, extended or opened to guests on a board that is internal only at COMMIT; random rounds of the three against the switch, and each stopped at its decision with the switch started against it (all 4 failed without the fix, including link creation's forced one).
    - **IO-6 (minor): two rules for a stale colleague.** An account confirmed more than 24 h ago whose stored address still qualifies counted as inside for the visibility rule (IO-2b, and IO-2a's "made private") but as not confirmed for keep sharing's grants, so a copy could say it left someone out while its visibility still reached them, and a raise went through for a member nobody had confirmed for a day (a former colleague whose company address was removed would be an outside account with that access at the next sign-in). Now one rule: only a confirmed creator is inside (`visibilityReachesOutside`: `NOT o_creator`). So that colleagues idle for a day do not block raises or make copies private, a raise in an internal-only scope (PATCH /api/boards/:bid, creator callers, after the route's checks) and a keep-sharing duplicate into one first confirm, outside the transaction, the stale or never-checked accounts the decision would count (`confirmStaleReach`: role `member` and grant holders of the board, not known outside, at most 20, four at a time, `refreshTarget`); whoever the provider cannot confirm counts as outside (fails closed). Cost: up to 20 provider calls on such a raise or copy, only for accounts stale for over a day. Tests: `internal-only-stale.test.ts` (Clerk fake: still a colleague, confirmed and copied; the address gone, refused and private; Clerk unavailable, refused and private and left out alike: 3 failed before); `internal-only-copies.test.ts`'s two dev cases with a never-checked creator now see him confirmed first.
    - **IO-7 (minor): under open email sign-in keep sharing left out a colleague who had not opened Lobby for a day** and called them not confirmed as Dark Burn Creative or Trailer Park Group (the email provider stamps the check only on a refresh).     - **IO-9 (major): a visibility raise in an internal-only scope was refused (409 `INTERNAL_ONLY`, "would reach people outside") when more than 20 colleagues had not opened Lobby for a day.** IO-6 made everyone not confirmed count as outside and `confirmStaleReach` confirmed at most 20, so the rest of a workspace of idle colleagues counted as outside until enough retries had confirmed them all (the review's run: 25 stale members, `[409, 200]`); "Duplicate, keep sharing" left them out or made the copy private as "not confirmed". The budget was also spent on accounts the decision does not count. Now: `confirmStaleReach` takes what the decision counts and confirms only those (a raise: members with role `member` whose own grant is below the new role; a copy: the source's grant holders and, unless it is private, the members), at most 50, five at a time, for at most 8 s (`staleConfirmLimits`, within a Clerk development instance's 100 requests per 10 s), and returns the ones it did not get to. When those are all that would refuse a raise (`visibilityReachesOutside` with them left out finds nobody), and for a copy whenever there are any, the answer is a new retryable **503 `COLLEAGUES_UNCONFIRMED`** ("Lobby is still confirming some colleagues' email addresses with the sign-in service, so nothing was changed. Try again in a minute."; `@draftroom/contracts` outside.ts), with nothing changed; each try confirms the next ones (for a day). Someone really outside still gets the 409 straight away, and an account the provider cannot confirm still counts as outside (IO-6's tests unchanged). The Share dialog and the dashboard show the server's words already. Kept the fail-closed rule rather than IO-2b's old "stale but qualifies is inside" (that is the former-colleague hole IO-6 closed). Not done: one batched provider call for many accounts (Clerk's `getUserList` by user id) would remove the budget; a later change. Tests: `internal-only-stale.test.ts` "IO-9" (budget 3 with 5 stale colleagues: the raise 503 then 200, the copy 503 with no board made then 201 keeping everyone, someone outside 409 at once, only the counted accounts asked, a provider slower than the time limit 503 then 200): 4 of the 5 failed before (the outside case is a guard).
    - **IO-10 (minor): export + import carried an internal-only project's stored files to a board outside it.** A creator who may not move a board out of such a project (IO-1) could export it, or write an archive naming its version ids, and import it into no project or a project that is not internal only: the import linked the project's originals to a board of their own outside the switch. Now `createBoardFrom` (import only), under the workspace row lock it already takes, leaves out every file the importer can view only on boards of an internal-only project when the new board lands outside every internal-only scope (`fenceInternalOnlyFiles`, history/files.ts); they show as missing, counted in `droppedFiles`, with the warning "N file(s) in this archive are on boards of an internal-only project, so they were not carried over to a board outside it and show as missing." (audit detail `filesFenced`). Into an internal-only project, while the workspace is internal only, by a workspace owner or admin who is a creator, or for a file the importer can also view on a board outside every internal-only project, files are carried as before. The archive's elements and words are not held back (the importer could read them anyway); the export route is unchanged (an archive holds only ids and hashes, never the files). `DOMAIN-ACCESS-CONTRACTS.md` §23 (IO-1 paragraph). Tests: `internal-only-import.test.ts` (an editor's import into no project and into an open project keeps none of the files and its board cannot serve them; into the project and by the owner they are carried; a switch-on committed right after the route's own file check is seen under the lock; a file also on an outside board is kept): 2 of the 4 failed before (the other two are guards).

Re-run below on real Postgres.

  - **Refix round 3 (a review of 58b492e3, two minor findings), as IO-11.** Each fix has tests that failed before it (run on the unfixed source first) and pass after.
    - **IO-11a (minor): after a 503 `COLLEAGUES_UNCONFIRMED`, the retry it asks for could be decided without asking the provider.** The calls still running at `confirmStaleReach`'s time limit then failed (Clerk's 10 s timeout, a 5xx or a 429), which puts each of those colleagues in the 60 s refresh-failure backoff; on the retry `refreshTarget` returned at once with no provider call and `confirmStaleReach` counted them as asked, so the raise got the outside 409 and a keep-sharing copy was made (201) private with their grants left out, for good. The same came straight away whenever Clerk answered 5xx or 429 on the first try. Access never leaked (fail closed), but the answer was wrong. Now `refreshTarget` resolves `false` when the provider could not answer (outcome `unavailable`, the backoff included, or a thrown error; `true` when the account is fresh already, has no provider check, or the provider answered; its other callers ignore it), and `confirmStaleReach` adds those accounts to `skipped`: the raise answers 503 when only they stand in the way, and the copy answers 503 before anything is made. The outside 409 stays for accounts the provider did answer about. No new machinery. Tests (`internal-only-stale.test.ts`): IO-6's "the provider cannot confirm her right now" now expects the 503 both ways with nothing changed; a new IO-9 case times the calls out, lets them fail into the backoff, heals the provider and retries: the raise and the copy are the 503 again (no board made), and after the backoff the raise passes. Both failed before (409).
    - **IO-11b (minor): the dashboard's import toast said the files IO-10 fenced were left out for "no access", and never showed the fence's warning.** `importedToast` (dashboard/model.ts) now shows every note the server sent (the fence, downloads off, no access, the archive's own notes), like `duplicatedToast`; with no note for left-out files (an older api), just the count, no reason guessed. Test: `model.test.ts` (failed before: no such helper; the old text was inline in `DashboardPage.tsx`).

No dependency or lockfile change (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` clean), no migration, no `apps/sync`, `apps/worker`, `packages/db` or `packages/canvas-schema` change, `CONTRACTS_REV` stays 11 and `LOBBY_CLIENT_REV` is unchanged.     - **K6, api reliability and cursor chat (api, contracts, web).** (1) The api's canvas probe starts `unknown` instead of `tldraw`, asks sync at start-up (250 ms, 500 ms, 1 s, 2 s, then every 5 s; one log line after 5 failed asks), and a request waits at most 2.5 s for an ask in flight. While unknown, tickets for new-canvas boards and every board creation answer **503 `CANVAS_STARTING`** with `Retry-After: 2` (never 409 `NEW_CANVAS`, never `new_canvas = false` on a guess); R0's display marks show only when sync is known to be tldraw; the asset collector deletes nothing. A sync answering 404 (R0) still reads as tldraw. The web retries `CANVAS_STARTING` on 250 ms..2 s. (2) Clerk telemetry off in code (`clerkClientOptions`, `clerkProviderOptions`). (3) `thumbnail.updated` on the board stream after an accepted upload or removal; the scheduler drops its pending picture when it hears another client's, waits the 2-minute gap plus 2-10 s of jitter outside the api's 30 s window and redraws first (two editors for 2 minutes: 0 thumbnail 429s). (4) Cursor chat: `/` on the board, a line of at most 80 characters floats at the sender's cursor for 5 s, then fades. `POST /api/boards/:bid/cursor-chat` (the comment action and up, an active board, authorship from the session; 5 per person and 60 per board per 10 s, 20 per guest link) relays `cursor.chat` on the stream and writes nothing. (5) Event, resync and access-lost listeners are isolated: one that throws is logged once (its kind and the error's class) and the stream stays open. Review: all good, 4 minors (two fixed below).

The dashboard, a sign-in on the way to a board and an invitation prefetch the board code at idle, and a pointer over, focus on or press of a board link at once (never on Save-Data or 2G). Stale-chunk recovery: one reload outside a board; on a board a notice with Reload that waits for unsent changes. The file-card occluder grid (0.70 ms against 7.45 ms per pass for 5,000 items).     2. K1-3 with K4-1: outside Save-Data and 2G every dashboard visitor downloads the board code at idle, and with it the template catalog (K4's gate then saves only its own idle load). Owner call: accept, or split the catalog out of the board page's static graph.
    3. K3-5: Present with colleagues on the board now goes live to everyone at once, with no confirmation (rehearsing in private is no longer possible). Owner call; tell the pilot users if it stays. (The race where Esc came first is fixed.)
    4. K1-4: the gates wait up to 1.5 s for the stream's hello before the first export-policy read; until then the policy counts as "everyone" for accounts, so a viewer on an export-off board may see export and copy entries a little longer (exports are refused by the api; clipboard copy is client-side). Accept and document, or fail closed for known viewers until the first answer.
    5. K2-4: the empty-board card and grid can mount for about one frame when a populated board opens (seen once at about 435 ms on a 2-note board); also require the first welcome before showing them.
    6. K2-5: the status dot's tone for the tldraw-era `unsupported` save state is "ok" (green). The Excalidraw engine never produces that state today, so nothing shows it; map it to warn with its label if it is ever used.

    8. K7: the AI popover grew by one or two rows and can sit under the selection bar or run off short viewports (no max-height); give `.dr-ai-pop` a max-height with scrolling and have its placement account for it.
    9. K7 with K2: the selection bar evaluates each item's `visible` only at render, so a read-only person who selects within about 2.5 s of the board going live gets no AI button until they select again; K2 can compute the visible items inside the `cux-selbar` selector.
    10. K6-1: `showsR0Marks` (display only) can wait up to 2.5 s for the engine while it is unknown (the dashboard list during a deploy with a slow sync); use the probe's `known()` there.

  - **Refix round 1 (a review of db71782c, app code 1ef3699d's: one major, two minor findings).** Each fix has a test that failed before it (the new unit tests run on the unfixed source first; the new e2e by patch-revert of `CanvasInput.ts`, restored) and passes after. No dependency, lockfile, `apps/sync` or migration change.
    1. **Major, K5-3: on Hand, two quick pan swipes edited the board.** Lobby's touch double-tap (`CanvasInput.onTouchStart`) ran the lease flow on any tool, so on a phone resting on Hand two quick swipes whose touchstarts paired (300 ms, 35 px) made a new synced free text, or opened a note's editor, while the person panned. Excalidraw itself does nothing there (`handleCanvasDoubleClick` returns for a tool that is not the selection tool, `dist/dev/index.js:44733`). Now, on a tool that is not the selection tool (or `autoshape`), the paired touchstart is still swallowed (the mirror stays exact) but nothing opens until the second touch ends as a tap after a first that did too (moved at most `TAP_SLOP_PX` 10 px, lasted at most `TAP_MAX_MS` 400 ms; `doubleTap.ts`); then a feature's double-tap or a note's lease flow runs, and a new free text is never made on Hand. Two real taps on a note on Hand still edit it (the hint's "Double-tap a note to edit it", K5-1). Tests: `CanvasInput.test.ts` 4 new (4 failed before); e2e `k5-phone` "K5-3 (round 1 refix)": two quick swipes from empty canvas and from a note make no element and open no editor, nothing pushed; two taps on empty canvas make nothing; two taps on the note open it (failed before: a new text's editor opened on the first swipe pair).
    2. **Minor, K1-7: offline on a board, a failed lazy chunk said "Lobby has been updated" and its Reload went to the offline page.** On a board, `report()` now shows a connection notice when the browser is offline ("This part of Lobby could not load while you are offline. Reload the page once you are back online to use it.", `stale-chunk-offline-notice`, same key, dismissable) with no Reload button. Off a board nothing changed. Test: `stale-chunk.test.ts` 1 new (failed before). Not done: the lazy part that failed still draws nothing in that tab until a reload (React.lazy keeps the fallback), as the notice says.
    3. **Minor, K6-4: a bubble not at its sender's cursor could pass for a colleague's.** A message from someone whose cursor this page does not show is placed at the `at` point the sender sends, which may be a colleague's cursor. Such a bubble (and one whose cursor left since) now starts with the sender's name from the server's event (`Name: text`, `cursor-chat-bubble-name`, `data-named`); a bubble at the sender's own live cursor is unchanged (its cursor label names them). `model.ts placeBubble`. Test: `model.test.ts` 2 new (failed before), including the reviewer's case (no cursor of the sender here, `at` = the colleague's cursor point: named). The markup is covered by the e2e only for the unnamed case (k6-cursor-chat, unchanged and passing).

Not run: the rest of the e2e suite, real phones.

No dependency, lockfile, `apps/sync` or migration change.
    1. **Minor, K1-7: offline, opening a board whose code failed to load said "Lobby has been updated" and "Reloading…", reloaded nothing, and its Reload went to the offline page.** Round 1 fixed this only on a board, but the board route is "off a board" while its code loads (no board has registered), so a colleague who lost signal and tapped a board card got the "updated" screen and toast; React.lazy then kept that screen for every board opened in the tab. Now `report()` shows the connection notice (`stale-chunk-offline-notice`, no Reload, no reload) whenever the browser is offline, on a board or not; `recoverChunk` takes an optional offline fallback, picked when the handler says the browser is offline; the board route's is `board-code-offline` ("You are offline… It opens by itself once you are back online.", no Reload), which reloads the page once the browser is back online (`reloadWhenOnline`: no board opened, so nothing is lost). The feedback page, the other lazy page outside a board, gets the same (`feedback-code-offline`). Online, nothing changed (a deploy's missing chunk still reloads once, guarded). Tests: `stale-chunk.test.ts` 5 new and the old off-board offline test rewritten (it pinned the "updated" notice with its Reload; 6 failed before); e2e `k1-stale-chunk` "offline (round 2 refix)": offline, a board card whose code cannot load shows the offline screen and notice, no "Lobby has been updated", no Reload, no reload; back online, one reload and the board opens (failed before: the snapshot showed "Lobby has been updated" / "Reloading to open this board…" and the toast with Reload).
    - **Checks on the final code** (port base 7700): `pnpm lint` (4 warnings, all in `apps/sync` tests, pre-existing), typecheck of `apps/web` and `e2e`, vitest `--project web --maxWorkers=4` **289 files, 3,083 tests passed**; e2e (chromium, `--workers=1`) `k1-stale-chunk` 4/4, `k1-prefetch` + `k1-skeleton` + `k1-open-data` + `feedback` 16/16.   - **Pre-deploy polish (K.1) (the lead's decisions on the owner's behalf; follow-ups 3 to 14 above, two K2 reviewer nits and a flaky spec; branch `wave-k/integration` from fe39d43d, still NOT merged to `main`, NOT deployed).** Each behaviour fix has a test shown to fail without it (new unit tests run on the unfixed source first, or by patch-revert of the app files in this worktree, restored after) and pass with it. No dependency, lockfile, `apps/sync` or migration change; `CONTRACTS_REV` stays 11.
    1. **P1, K3-5 (follow-up 3): Present asks first.** With other people on the board (and this person may present to everyone, and nobody does yet), Present and Ctrl/Cmd+Alt+Enter open "Present to everyone?" with "Present to everyone (N here)" (the default: it has the focus, Enter takes it) and "Just me" (present mode in this tab only; "Invite everyone" stays in the bar). Escape, × or a click outside starts nothing; nothing goes live to anyone without the choice. Alone, as a viewer, while someone presents, or with nothing to present: no question (`live/ported/controller.ts offerAudience`, `ported/controller.ts presentChoice` / `presentTo`, `PresentOverlay.tsx PresentChoiceDialog`; the dialog closes and gives the focus back before present mode starts, in the same gesture, so full screen still works). N counts the other people (two tabs of one person are one). Tests: `controller.test.ts` 2 new; e2e `k3-live` "K.1" (the question, Escape, Just me with no toast for bob and carol, Enter to everyone with bob's toast; failed before at the question) and "alone, no question". The specs that present now use `present-kit.ts startPresenting` (it answers "to everyone", what Present did at once before; 12 spec files). This replaces the deploy note "Present with colleagues on the board now presents to everyone at once": tell the pilot users Present asks.
    2. **P2, K1-4 (follow-up 4): the export policy fails closed until its first answer.** One rule for every gate on the board page (`sharing/export-rules.ts boardExportAllowed`, used by sharing, canvas-ux's menus and Ctrl/Cmd+C, present-export, History, the vote CSV, the mind-map outline and the file cards): before the answer only the owner and editors who are not name-only guests get export and copy-out entries. **One deviation from the brief, on purpose:** it said "anyone who is not the owner"; editors with an account are allowed by every policy (`exportAllowedFor`), so hiding theirs would only have flashed them off and blocked their Ctrl+C for a second on every open; they see no change, like the owner. While the policy is unknown nothing claims exporting is off: the menus drop the entries without the "turned off" line, and a blocked copy or export says "Checking whether you can export or copy on this board. Try again in a moment." (`export-pending-notice`). Tests: `export-pending.test.ts` (4, failed before), the old "unknown is allowed" assertions updated in 6 test files; e2e `sharing-export-control` "K.1" (a viewer whose export-policy read is held: no Export submenu, no Copy or Export as, no off line, the pending notice; the entries come with the answer; failed before at the Export submenu).
    3. **P3, K2-4 (follow-up 5): no start-card flash.** Cause found: features mount at the first welcome, but a joiner within 5 s of another open gets the room's cached reset snapshot and the rows since it as a `changes` frame right behind, so a populated board read as empty in between (the probe: 6 of 6 fresh opens of a 2-note board mounted the card for a moment). The card and the grid now wait for the room's first welcome AND its catch-up: the board session marks the canvas ready on the room's `contracts` frame, which follows them (or 1.5 s after the first welcome for a room that sends none; `canvas/api/room-ready.ts`, `session.ts`). A failed `?template=` insert now drops the param, so the start card shows ("Browse templates" is one click away); the Try again notice still inserts it, and the tab is marked only when an insert lands (LEAD-2 kept the param, which left the board blank behind the hidden card). Tests: `empty-board-hint.test.ts` 2 new, `start-view.test.ts` updated (failed before); e2e `k2-empty-board` "K.1" (six fresh opens of a board with notes: no card or grid ever mounts; then emptied, both show; failed before on the first open).
    4. **P4, K2-5 (follow-up 6):** the `unsupported` save state takes the warning tone with its words ("Live"), never green (`topbar-fit.ts`; `topbar-status.test.ts` 1 new, failed before).
    5. **P5, K2-1 (follow-up 7): the focus ring follows native `:focus-visible`.** Any key that moves the focus or acts (arrows, Delete, Enter, Space, a tool's letter, Tab, F6) makes the keyboard the last input and shows the ring at once when the board has the focus; Escape, a modifier alone, Ctrl/Meta/Alt chords, dead and IME keys and typing in a field or the note editor do not (`focus-ring.ts keyboardIntent`). A pointer user's edit then Escape still shows no ring; a keyboard user who clicked once and works by keys now gets it (WCAG 2.4.7). Type-to-edit's first letter on a selected note is the keyboard acting, so it shows the ring after its Escape, as Chrome's own `:focus-visible` would. Tests: `focus-ring.test.ts` (4 new or rewritten); e2e `k2-focus-ring` rewritten (the edit by double-click, Escape alone, then an arrow and a tool letter show the ring and a pointer move hides it; failed before at the arrow).
    6. **P6, K7 (follow-ups 8 and 9):** the AI popover gets a max-height and scrolls inside it; it goes below its anchor when it fits, else above, else on the roomier side, never over the selection bar (its anchor is the whole bar now) and never off a short window (`ai/ported/placement.ts`, `placement.test.ts` 6). The selection bar evaluates each item's `visible` inside its selector (`SelectionBar.tsx selectionBarView`), so a viewer who selects before the AI status arrives gets the AI button when it does. e2e `k7-ai-mindmap` "K.1" (3: the viewer's button without reselecting; the popover at 1280×520 and 900×440, off the bar and inside the canvas, its last action reachable; all 3 failed before).

A device with no cursor (`(any-hover: hover)` false: a touch screen alone) is not offered cursor chat: its key does nothing and the shortcuts dialog has no row (`CommandSpec.available`, new and additive). Tests: `keys.test.ts` 2 new (failed before); e2e `k6-cursor-chat` 2 new (a CDP key event as the German layout sends it reaches bob; a phone's `/` opens nothing; both failed before).

Before: 5, 4 and 1 of 20 failed (three runs of `--repeat-each=20`); after: 20/20.

    1. **K5-1 (major): the phone edit zoom now goes back when the keyboard resizes the canvas during the move.** On Android Chrome (`interactive-widget=resizes-content`) the soft keyboard shrinks the canvas while the 320 ms move still runs; the camera tween keeps the centre fixed, so the move ended about 150 screen px from its target and the close took that for the person's pan and left the camera at 100 %. `edit-zoom.ts` now listens to the canvas's `resize`: a resize during its own move re-takes "where this put it" from where that move settles (when it reached the zoom it aimed for; a pinch that interrupted it still counts as the person's), and a resize during the move back puts the camera exactly where it was once that move settles (without animation). A resize while nothing moves changes nothing (the engine keeps the top-left). Still a camera move only: nothing is pushed (the e2e counts). Tests: `edit-zoom.test.ts` 7 new (3 failed before; the 4 controls passed before and after); e2e `a11y-phone` "K5-1 (review refix)" (a viewport resize 80 ms into the move, then Escape: before, the camera stayed at zoom 1 instead of the fit; a resize 250 ms after Escape: before, 150 px off; both steps failed before, each run alone).
    2. **K5-1 (minor): the comment no longer says the move gives way while presenting** (nothing did; `CameraMove.force` is never read). It now says what happens: the tap that opens an editor ends a follow, and a presenter's followers see the presenter's edit zoom like any other move of the presenter's camera.
    3. **K5-5 (minor): a new link's note says nothing about the automatic copy until it settles.** The email-locked link from Invite people and the one-person link from Guests and clients showed "Couldn't copy automatically" in their `role=status` note while `copyBounded` was still waiting (up to 1.5 s), then "Copied.". Both now keep a tri-state (null = pending, as `CopyBoardLink` and `EmailInvite` do) through `share-copy.ts copyNote`. Tests: `share-copy.test.ts` 1 new; e2e `k5-share` "K5-5 (review refix)" (a copy that never answers: nothing about it at first, the failure after the timeout; both steps failed before).

The K5-4 menu test starts from a clean inbox (it depended on test order) and checks the menu again with an unread mention (failed before: "Notifications, 1 unread" beside the badge "1").

Web only: no dependency, lockfile, `apps/sync`, api or migration change; `CONTRACTS_REV` stays 11.
    1. **K1-4 / K.1 fail-closed export gate (major; the minor "never retries" finding is the same defect): a failed first export-policy read is retried.** Since K.1 a viewer, commenter or name-only guest gets no export, copy or download entry until the board's policy answer arrives, and the pending notice says "Try again in a moment". When that one read failed (a 5xx or a timeout in the open burst) while the event stream stayed up, nothing read again until a reconnect, so those people stayed locked out for the session. `sharing/ported/export-policy.ts` now retries a failed read while a board session is live and there is no answer yet, after `EXPORT_POLICY_RETRY_MS` (1 s, 3 s, then every 10 s), stopped by an answer, by any other read (an event, a resync, a sharing change) and when the last reader detaches. A board with an answer is not retried (it keeps what it had, as before). Test: `export-policy-resource.test.ts` "a failed FIRST read is retried with backoff" (failed before: one read, the viewer still closed after the first delay); it also checks the backoff, that an answer ends the retries and that a session that leaves reads nothing more.
    2. **K3-5 Present choice (minor): a held Enter no longer goes live to everyone.** Enter on the Present button opens the question with "Present to everyone" focused; the key repeat of that same held Enter then pressed it. The dialog now ignores repeated Enter and Space keydowns (window capture, `preventDefault`) while it is open: only a fresh press chooses, and Enter stays the default. Test: e2e `k3-live` "K.1 refix: Enter held down on Present" (Playwright marks the second `keyboard.down` as a repeat; before, the question closed and the presentation started).
    3. **K2-1 / K.1 focus ring (minor): no ring for a pointer user after cursor chat or type-to-edit, and no flicker while Space-dragging to pan.** `a11y/ported/focus-ring.ts`: characters typed on the board (no Ctrl, Meta or Alt; not Space) remember the modality before them, and when the focus then lands in a text entry (cursor chat's composer, a note's editor) they were typing, so that modality comes back; any other input ends the run. Key auto-repeats are ignored (`index.tsx` passes `KeyboardEvent.repeat`; it also tells the rule when a focus lands in a text entry). A keyboard user who types to edit still gets the ring back, a tool letter that opens nothing still shows it, and Enter to edit is still the keyboard acting. Tests: `focus-ring.test.ts` 4 new (3 failed before: cursor chat, type-to-edit, the Space-pan trace); e2e `k2-focus-ring` 2 new steps, type-to-edit then Escape (restoring the K2-1 path; failed before: one ring) and cursor chat "/", a message, Enter (no ring).

View runs the panel summary and recap, ask and find; edit the rest. A run id per call (`air_` + 20 base62, never stored by the api), ephemeral `ai.working` / `ai.done` board events (after the gate and the reservation; done in a finally; nothing for a refused call). The probe: the room's contracts frame says 11; neither tab sends `t` or `trail` with the laser in hand and no socket closes; the Frames panel offers no minutes stepper; edits save both ways, no rejection, no page error.
    - **(c) A rev-11 tab (the deployed web) and a rev-12 tab on one board, the merged api and the rev-12 sync** (one web origin in front of both builds, so the api's and sync's Origin checks hold): edits both ways saved with no rejection, cursors both ways, the rev-12 tab's laser (it sends `t`; the rev-11 tab never sends `t` and never receives `t` or `trail`), follow both ways, a vote cast from both and revealed (ranks on both), cursor chat both ways, a spotlight (the rev-11 tab keeps working), a restore-elements by the rev-12 side reaching the rev-11 tab as ordinary changes; no page error on either. **One incompatibility found (major, a deploy hold): the deployed web drops every server frame that carries `present.mins`.** Its socket parses each JSON frame with the strict `ServerMessage`, and a patch op's `c` (`LobbyPatch`) checks each value with its own `PresentValue`, which has no `mins`; the whole `changes` frame is dropped with only a log line (`server_frame_invalid`). So a rev-11 tab (1) misses every minutes write and anything committed with it (W5's reorder and skip write every frame's `present` together), and (2) after a reconnect, misses its whole catch-up when a frame's minutes changed meanwhile: in the probe a note moved while the tab was offline never reached it (20 s, the tab showing Online and Saved); without the minutes write in that window it caught up (the control). Moves of a frame that has minutes reach it (a patch without `c`), a fresh open or a reload heals it (the snapshot is not schema-checked: it then shows the minutes), and the server's data is intact. W6's stale-pair tests did not see it: they ran the current web core behind a rev-11 shim, which does not check incoming `customData`. **Corrected, and fixed (follow-up 1 fix, below):** a reconnect's catch-up is whole elements (`c` ops), which the deployed web takes (re-probed with its real build and this sync before the fix: an offline window with a minutes write and a separate move ended converged), and the snapshot is schema-checked too and passes for the same reason; what a rev-11 tab missed for good was a LIVE frame (the probe's minutes and move were one frame), whose rows come back only if the tab reconnects before a later frame moves its clock past them.

Server data is safe and a reload heals the tab. Options: (1) deploy the web right after the sync in the quiet window and have every pilot user reload open boards before anyone sets minutes in the Frames panel (W5's run of show) or reorders frames that have them; (2) ship the web with the minutes stepper hidden and switch it on in a later web deploy, once no deployed-web tab can be open; (3) a sync change before shipping: a reconnecting socket whose catch-up would carry a `present.mins` value gets the room's snapshot instead (the snapshot path is not schema-checked by the deployed web), which heals case 2 but not case 1. Recommended: (1), with (3) as the wave M fix and, for every later bump, a stale-pair test that parses the new room's frames with the previous build's own `ServerMessage`.

  - **Round 1 refix (the integration review: 2 major, 4 minor; all six verified on c0df753e and fixed, each with a test that failed first).**
    1. **Major, "Undo restore" revived a colleague's later deletion and took back their later edits** (two findings, one cause: the undo applied the `before_restore` checkpoint wholesale, and the web's ids were the asked ones, not what the restore wrote). "Undo restore" now names the restore it undoes: `restoreClock` (that restore's answered `clock`, required with `removeAbsent` in `RestoreElementsInput` and `RestoreElementsBody`; every server write has a clock of its own). The sync takes back a unit only when that restore wrote it (an element of it stored at that clock) and nobody wrote it since: an element stored above that clock, or one the undo would bring back that anything but that restore deleted, skips the unit with the new code **`CHANGED`**. A unit the restore did not write (already as the checkpoint had it, or changed between the checkpoint and the write) is left as it is. The web sends it (`time-travel.ts undoRestoreInput`) and says "N objects someone changed since". Tests: sync `restore-elements` "Undo restore is an undo" (Ann restores N1, N2, N3 and an unchanged R; Bob deletes N1 and R and moves N3; the undo leaves all three, takes back only N2, and leaves a text changed in the window; failed before: no skips, Bob's deletion revived); contracts 2 new, api 1 changed (400 without `restoreClock`, passed on with it), web 1 new; all failed before (the contracts ones by a patch-revert).
    2. **Minor, removals by restore-elements were "Recently deleted" by Lobby.** `applyRestoreElements` writes the `lb_deleted` row itself with the restorer as `deletedBy` (`ServerWriteDeletedView.deletedSession` on the server write's tx; the room then adds no row of its own). Test: the sync undo test reads `/internal/boards/:bid/deleted` (failed before: `lobby-server`).
    3. **Minor, re-sorting deleted old headings that were locked or in use.** `plan.ts staleHeadings` keeps a heading the note skip rules (`model.ts skipOf`) would leave (locked, leased or this tab's editor, dragged by someone else); it stays as an obstacle. Test: `run.test.ts` "never deletes a heading that is locked, being typed in or dragged" (failed before: delete ops for all three).
    4. **Minor, Gather with "Move the originals" moved a note someone was typing in or dragging.** `candidatesOf` with `move` also leaves a winner whose element or label is leased (or this tab's editor) or dragged by someone else, counted with the locked ones; the toast says "locked, in use, a frame or gone". Copying is unchanged. Test: `vote-board.test.ts` 1 new (failed before: 3 gathered, the typed-in and dragged notes moved).
    5. **Minor, handing the presentation off and taking it back wiped the run of show and restarted the section timer.** `mountAgenda` gets `presentationId` (the controller's `store.presentation`; a hand-off keeps the id): the same presentation taken back carries its record on, and the section it left is not a new visit, so no second timer start. A new presentation still starts afresh. Test: `agenda-live.test.ts` 1 new (failed before: Warm-up's minutes gone and a third timer start); the "new run" test now gives the second run its own id. Left: a reload while handed away starts a new run (the id is kept in memory, not in the record; follow-up 6 stands).

By diff (`packages/canvas-schema/src/reader-rule.test.ts`): no message type, reject or notice code, close reason, field group, wire limit, element type or element field changed since 11; the `customData` registry (keys, classes, sizes, duplicate and lock rules) is the same, and its one value change is `present.mins`; presence gained `t` and `trail`. Parsed in every frame kind, the 437b4e7 parse **drops** a `changes` frame (a live batch, or the one after a cached snapshot) whose `p` op carries `present.mins` in its `c`, with every op of it, and a presence entry with `t` or `trail` (the room never sent those to it: W6's `presenceForReader`). It **accepts** `present.mins` in a whole element (a create, a reconnect's catch-up rows, a server repair, a refusal's stored copy, the gzip snapshot) and in an ack's fix (`c:present`), `contracts` 12, every other message type with every code and reason, and every other wave L shape in every frame: AI provenance with run ids, the recap's, diagram's, flow's, sort's, 2x2's, pieces' and vote results' kinds, tags, W7's restore writes (the room test adds the 22 real pieces of `@draftroom/templates`). The writer side: the 437b4e7 Frames panel writes `present` whole as `{index, skip}` or null, so its reorder, skip and auto-order erased the minutes (L-1 had pinned that); `ai` and `kind` are create-only, so its edits never touch provenance, run ids or kinds.
    - **The fix** (EXCALIDRAW-CONTRACTS §0b and L-2, AGENTS.md's stale-client sentence). (1) canvas-schema `frameForReader(reader, frame, stored)`, one function for every server message type: for a reader below 12 a `p` op whose `c` carries a field of `LOBBY_FIELD_ADDITIONS` becomes the `c` op of the element as stored, which loses nothing (the rev-11 tab's copy stays the server's exactly: its hash, its lost-push decisions and its undo compare against it; a tombstone's patch loses the field instead), presence goes through `presenceForReader`, every other type is unchanged. (2) The sync applies it at `Session.send`, the room's one send point (`apps/sync/src/room/reader-frames.ts`): every frame is a `MessageFrame` serialized once per revision, or one of the two pre-serialized paths that are in their reader's form by construction (catch-up `c` ops; the presence hub's per-form frames); a test scans the room for any other write. (3) The connect URL names the client's revision (`rev`; the web, the headless client and the spike page send it, a rev-11 sync ignores it), so the room knows it before the welcome and before a welcome's replayed pushes; without it a socket is read as 11 until it sends presence `t`. (4) A push carries its socket's revision, and a rev-11 writer's `present` keeps the stored `mins` in the same commit (patches, and a create over a tombstone); its removal keeps `{mins}` alone; the ack's fix names the stored value, so the writer's undo folds it (its Ctrl+Z of an auto-order still restores the order, with the minutes). A rev-12 write is taken as written (the stepper's "No time planned" clears them).

New functions: `reader-rule.test.ts` 24, `reader-frames.test.ts` 4, `connect-query.test.ts` 2. `present-mins.test.ts` and `stale-pairs-rev12.test.ts` now pin the keep (they pinned the erasure).
